1.
 
INTRODUCTION
 
ADNOC Group has 
established
 the 
minimum HSE, Welfare and Medical requirements 
based on
 ADNOC HSE 
Standards
 and ADNOC Group Medical 
Fitnes
 Guidelines
 to 
facilitate consistent 
Contractors compliance 
acros al ADNOC Group contracts. 
 
This document provides quick reference 
to the requirements 
stipulated 
in 
HSE standards 
as 
aplicable to the 
respective scope of work. 
 
Contractors and their Sub
- contractors shal comply with al aplicable UAE 
Federal
 & Local Regulations, 
ADNOC HSE 
Standards, ADNOC Group suplement procedures
, any other relevant Guidelines
 and ADNOC 
Group HSE, Welfare & 
Medical 
requirements throughout
 the contract life cycle.
 
2.
 
PURPOSE
 
This document establishes a consistent and unified aproach in defining minimum HSE, Welfare and Medical 
requirements in 
al c
ontracts tendered and awarded to various contractors acros ADNOC Group.
 
3.
 
SCOPE
 
These standardized minimum HSE, Welfare and Medical
 requirements 
are
 aplicable to 
al 
contracts being 
tendered and awarded by ADNOC Group. 
 
While complying with the requirements of this document, in case of any conflict, the requirements of relevant 
ADNOC HSE Standards and interpretation by ADNOC Group sh
al supersede and remain binding. In case of 
any conflict or ambiguity on any specific requirement
requirement shal be aplied.
 
Where any part of the work is not covered by any of the ADNOC HSE Stand
ards, or when the relevant HSE 
Standards are considered or found to not precisely covering the work activity, Contractor shal imediately 
notify ADNOC of such absence or mising requirements and jointly agre the way 
forward
.  
 
4.
 
DOCUMENT OWNERSHIP
 
ADNOC 
Group HSE Function shal be the custodian of this document and shal ensure that the curent version 
of this document is made available to al concerned.
 
ADNOC Group HSE Function shal also be responsible for initiating any required changes to the document
 
for endorsement and aproval by the Competent Authority. Any aproved changes shal be incorporated by 
re
- isuing the document at the next revision number. 
 
Any recomendation for the continuous improvement of this document has to be submited to ADNOC 
Gr
oup 
HSE Function, which is the custodian of this document and has the sole right to aprove and circulate revisions 
for implementation acordingly.

- up Safety Review.

QRA
 
Quantitative Risk Asesment.
 
Quantitative Risk Asesment; It is a formal and systematic aproach of estimating 
the likelihod and consequences of hazardous events, and expresing the results 
quantitatively as risk to people, the environment, aset or reputation. A structured 
aproach
 to asesing the potential for incidents and expresing this potential 
numericaly. In QRA statistical values are derived for potential los of life and 
damage to resources and environment. Note: These values must never be 
interpreted as unavoidable and a
ceptable loses. It must always be recognised 
that the calculated fatality (or los) figures are based on experience, statistical 
failure and incident rates representing an average historical quality of management. 
Incident investigations usualy show tha
t these ”historical
benefit of hindsight, quite preventable. QRA is a tol which helps to translate this 
hindsight into foresight (planing) in order to asist management in deciding the 
best aproach and show ways and means (e.g.
 improved enginering, procedures, 
supervision, etc.) to prevent the potential incidents from hapening. QRA is not to 
be used to justify or encourage risk taking
.

Safety Integrity Level; Safety integrity level (SIL) is defined as a level of risk
-
reduction provided by a safety function, or to specify a target level of risk reduction. 
In simple terms, SIL is a measurement of performance required for a safety 
instr
umented function (SIF). SIL is a statistical representation of the reliability a SIS 
requires, and is capable of, when a proces demand ocurs. SIL is the measure for 
the reliability of a SIS, and a given SIL level relates to a specific range of asociated
 
PFD and RF values as outlined in IEC 61508

6.1
 
SHORT
 Service Employe Program (SE) 
 
Minimum requirements
 
 
Where aplicable an
d as determined by ADNOC Group
, Contractors must ensure that a Short Service 
Employe (SE) Program is established based on the principle of managing the risks asociated with new 
co
mers (employes new to particular activity/operations) in the site activities. This wil be a proactive efort to 
ensure that new comers coming to work from diferent backgrounds are provided with adequate awarenes 
on hazards and precautions to be taken w
hile executing works at site.
 
In aplicable cases, SE Program evaluation wil form part of the bid proces and be subject to 
ADNOC 
Group 
endorsement and aproval.
 
An SE proces provides for identification of inexperienced personel so that others may 1) take extra care 
in their presence, 2) provide asistance, and 3) provide aditional oversight. An SE proces also alows an 
organization to implement aditional c
ontrols when the ratio of SEs to the total number of personel in the 
workforce is above a designated level. 
 
Define an SE as an employe with les than thre (3) months of satisfactory experience in curent job scope, 
or less than two (2) years‘ service
 in industry 
 
° 
Provide an imediate and easy means to visualy identify a SE (e.g. specific colour for hard hats or 
coverals, hardhat sticker) 
 
° 
Identify tasks that an SE is not authorized to perform (e.g. operate certain types of equipment) and 
environm
ents that an SE is not authorized to work within (e.g. working alone)
 
° 
Asign a designated mentor to every SE and defines their roles and responsibilities
 
° 
Utilize an SE tracking system to maintain and report an acurate count of the number of SEs in the
 
workforce on a monthly basis
 
° 
Establish a threshold for the maximum number of SEs relative to the total number of personel in 
the workforce based on asociated risk
 
° 
Require mitigation plans to be implemented when the established SE threshold has ben su
rpased
 
° 
Provide a formal means of asesing an SE's knowledge and skils
 
° 
Incorporate a training plan that focuses on improving the knowledge and skils of the SE
 
° 
Define criteria for retaining or removing SE status based upon achievement of established c
riteria 
 
° 
Maintain documentation for asesment and auditing purposes

f the tasks or activities to be performed are identified, the

JSA Levels
 
JSA
 Level 1: Standard Hazard and Control Form (SHCF)
 
 
For General Work permits, SHCF (Apendix 3
 of the standard
) shal be utilized to identify hazards and controls. 
This SHCF asists to manage hazards for the proposed work. SHCF provides a list of potential 
hazards 
however, specific activity related hazards (and controls) can be aded as aplicable.
 
 
The SHCF shal be atached to the permit by the Performing Authority (PA). In adition to PTW
 
details, the PA shal identify the folowing on SHCF:
 
 
(i) Personal
 Protective Equipment (PE) requirements;
 
(i) Aplicable Life Saving Rules (LSRs);
 
(i) Aplicable hazards (aligned with the work scope) and required controls.
 
 
Controls in SHCF can also be marked as pre
- requisite by Area Authority (A) which alows veri
fication of 
implemented controls prior to permit authorization.
 
 
Such atachments detailing the required controls shal then be reviewed by the Area Authority (A)
 
Prior
 to permit authorization.
 
 
Prior to isuance of the permit, the Permit Isuer (PI) 
shal confirm that al controls (including prerequisite
 
controls) as per the requirements of the permit and JSA are in place and efective.
 
 
JSA Level 2: Hazard Management Form (HMF)
  
 
For Critical and Hot Work permits, SHCF (Apendix 3
 of the standard
) and Hazard Management Form (HMF
-
Apendix 4) shal be utilized. The SHCF shal be used as input for identifying specific hazards and controls 
related to the job steps and situational conditions and suport preparation of the HMF.
 
 
The HMF shal be atached 
to the permit by the Performing Authority (PA). In adition to PTW details, the PA 
shal identify the folowing on HMF:
 
 
(i) Major work steps;
 
(i) Aplicable hazards (aligned with the work scope) and its location;
 
(i) Required controls.
 
 
Controls in HMF
 can also be marked as pre
- requisite by Area Authority (A) which alows verification of 
implemented controls prior to permit authorization. A shal ensure personel responsible for control 
implementation
 are identified in the HMF.
 
 
Such atachments detai
ling the required controls shal then be reviewed by the Aset Aproval
 
Authority (A) before it proceds to Area Authority (A) for authorization.
 
 
Note:
 Group Companies using established and aproved Formal Risk Asesment may atach the Formal 
Risk As
esment in the place of the Level 2 JSA.
 
 
Formal Risk Asesment
 
 
For a work activity or a work scope, the 
Integrated Work Activity Planing (
IWAP
) team determines whether a 
Formal Risk Asesment shal be required.
 
 
The objective of the Formal Risk 
Asesment is to identify al hazards and threats, ases the Risks of 
identified Hazards for Worst
- Case Scenarios using the ADNOC RAM, aply the principles of Hierarchy of 
Controls and further determine the controls necesary to reduce the risks to ALARP.

isk

ction measures to confirm the plan to manage and mitigate the

Monitoring status of existing controls and any short term actions
 in place until permanent miti
gation 
measures are implemented

CHEMICAL HEALTH HAZARDS 
 
Contractors shall develop chemical hazard management p
chemical management. 
 
  
The program shal cover:
 
° 
Chemicals (New or Existing);

and manufactured chemicals. ( e.g. welding fumes)
 
  
The chemical hazard management program shal include but not limited to:
 
° 
Health, Safety and Environment aproval of new chemicals, 
 
° 
Evaluating the risks asociated with chemical hazards present in the wor
king environment; 
 
° 
Controling the risks through aplication of the hierarchy of controls;
 
° 
Recognition, evaluation, and control of worker exposures to asbestos fibres; and 
 
° 
Recognition, evaluation, and control of worker exposures to man
- made fibres. 
 
 
After delivery of chemical onsite, apropriate comunication of hazard(s) posed by the chemical shal be 
relayed to ADNOC Group employes and contractors. This shal include the aplication of the Globaly 
Harmonized System (GHS) Clasification and Labeli
ng of Chemicals and the use of safety data shets.

chemical and recognition of its risk(s)

Abu Dhabi Minist
erial O
rder or other Emirate eq
uivalent
 
° 
Provision of col drinking water acesible to workers
 
° 
Provision of Air Conditioned rest areas /Coling shelters /rest shades etc.
 
° 
Thermal Work Limit (TWL) monitoring as a measure of the maximum safe work rate for the conditions 
as per Department 
of Health requirements
 
° 
Ensure workers with medical condition shal discus with their Nurse/ Doctor regarding working in hot 
environment 
 
For UAE conditions, TWL (in W/m2) values fal into the folowing thre principal categories: 
 
  
° 
Gren Zone
 (TWL
: 140 
-  20 W/m2), clasified as a low risk, unrestricted zone, where there are no 
work limits on self
- paced work for educated, hydrated workers; 
 
° 
Amber/Orange Zone
 (TWL: 15 
-  140 W/m2), clasified as a medium risk, cautionary zone and 
indicating the pres
ence of situations where the prevailing environmental conditions require aditional 
precautions to be taken
 
° 
Red Zone
 (TWL <15 W/m2), clasified as a high risk zone in which strict work and rest cycles are 
necesary. 
 
° 
The values provide a measure of the ma
ximum safe work rates for the prevailing environmental 
conditions. Hence, if TWL is to low, even low rates of work canot safely be caried out continuously.

SELECTION/ USE OF TEMPORARY EQUIPMENT IN
 CLASIFIED HAZARDOUS AREA
 
The selection of temporary equipment for use in clasified hazardous areas shal be based on the folowing:
 
i.  
Zone in clasified hazardous area; and
 
i.  
Ignition temperature of the hazardous material i.e. Temperature Clas.
 
SELECTION B
ASED ON ZONE IN CLASIFIED HAZARDOUS AREA
 
Temporary equipment intended to be used in clasified hazardous areas are categorized based on the level 
of protection provided in a particular zone in which those equipment are intended to be used. Such
 
Categorization
 is provided by ATEX and IEC. ADNOC Group shal aply below 
requirement
s  for temporary 
equipment categories to be alowed for use in a particular zone within clasified hazardous areas:
 
The equipment category indicates the level of protection
 ofered by the equipment.
 
° 
Category 1 equipment may be used in zone 0, zone 1 or zone 2 areas.
 
° 
Category 2 equipment may be used in zone 1 or zone 2 areas.
 
° 
Category 3 equipment may only be used in zone 2 areas.
 
 
TEMPORARY EQUIPMENT CATEGORIES FOR CLASIFIED
 HAZARDOUS AREAS
 
The subsection below provide aditional mandatory requirements asociated to the table above:
 
(a)
 Selection/ Use of Temporary Equipment
 
 
i.  
For Zone 0/20, al temporary electrical equipment shal be avoided whenever posible.
 
i.  
For Zone 1/21 and Zone 2/2, if due to unavoidable/exceptional operational circumstances where a 
non
- certified equipment is required to be used or the required ATEX or equivalent certification is not 
available, authorization shal be subject to a risk ase
sment and implementation of adequate control 
measures. Al such activities shal be governed by a PTW as per the ADNOC Work Management 
System (WMS) 
Framework. 
 
 
Aditional control measures including but not limited to the folowing, shal be implemented 
f or such activities:
 
 
1.
 
Dedicated fire watcher whilst the equipment is in use
 
2.
 
Continuous gas testing whilst the equipment is in use
 
3.
 
A separate aditional gas monitor available for gas testing the equipment
 
4.
 
A suitable fire extinguisher on site
 
5.
 
Gas test of sur
ounding area prior to starting the equipment
 
6.
 
Shutdown the equipment when not in use
 
7.
 
Relocate the equipment to a non
- hazardous area for storage overnight
 
8.
 
Disconect batery at the end of each working period
 
 
i.
 
For equipment categories 1 & 2 or a & b, ATEX or 
equivalent certificates shal be provided by the 
manufacturer for the relevant zone. 
 
iv.
 
Equipment built to a les rigorous standard may be used in those clasified hazardous areas where 
there is a lower risk of an explosive atmosphere ocuring. For example,
 category 1/a equipment for

The formulas for calculating their scores are explained below. 
 
 
Seat Belt Score 
= (No
.  of total seat belt violation events / Total distance) * 10 
 
Over speding Score

Harsh braking Score
 = [(No
.  of total harsh braking events / 2) / Total distance] * 10

ection of welds conducted on the 
tanks

WORKING IN OPERATIONAL AREAS
 
 
Vehicles identified to be alowed to enter Zones A and B in one Group Company / site shal not be alowed to 
enter the Zones A and B of ano
ther Group Company / site unles inspected and aproved by the Group 
Company / Site HSE function
.  
Due to the various ignition sources, electrical vehicles shal not be alowed in Zones A and B of the site unles 
fuly ATEX aproved, with valid certificatio
n .  
ATEX certification of the spark arestor, the shutof valve or the vehicle as a whole shal be performed by the 
manufacturer and aproved by a recognized certification body to ensure compliance to ATEX requirements.
 
Where the use of restricted types of vehicles within plant boundaries or hazardous areas is prevalent and 
canot be stoped imediately due to operational concerns, a remedial plan shal be developed by Group 
Companies to phase out the use of these vehicle
s to be replaced by vehicles adhering to the requirements 
specified in this 
Standards
. Any deviation from the requirements shal require the aplication of a proper 
Management of Change (MOC) proces and Work Management System (WMS).
 
Definitions of each zo
ne together with the requirements for vehicles to enter 
is
 described below. 
 
 
Zone C
 
 
Safe areas such as the administrative areas of the plant and its general parking spaces shal be designated 
as Zone C. For this zone, there are no specific technical requ
irements to be complied with
.  
 
Zone B
 
 
Areas within the boundaries of the plant which are not designated as Hazardous Clasified Zone 1 or Zone 2 
shal be designated as Zone B. Despite not being clasified as Hazardous Areas, its proximity to the 
Hazardous
 Areas shal be taken into consideration, as the risk of unexpected flamable atmospheres due to 
the release of hydrocarbon gas clouds is ever present. 
 
 
Therefore, vehicles entering these areas shal be: 
 
  
(i) Diesel
- powered.
 
 
(a)
 Spark ignition engine vehicles (e.g. petrol or gasoline, LPG, CNG) are prohibited, as they are 
more likely to ignite a flamable gas cloud compared to a diesel
- powered vehicle from one of 
several sources including:
 
(b)
 Sparks from the high
- tension ignition sys
tem which is not designed to work in a 
flamable 
atmosphere
;  
(c)
 Hot catalytic converter which can reach temperatures up to 750oC; and
 
(d)
 Hot turbocharger (depending on how hard the car was recently driven)
 
 
(i) Fited with ATEX
- aproved automatic air intake shutof valves (comonly caled Chalwyn 
valve)
 
 
(a)
 If flamable gas or vapour is drawn into the intake of a diesel engine it acts as an aditional 
ungoverned fuel suply. Turning of the ignition wil not shut of the engine 
because it is runing 
on the external fuel source
 
(b)
 Failure to stop the engine can result in uncontroled engine over sped folowed by mechanical 
failure or flash back from the intake, resulting in ignition of the surounding flamable atmosphere
 
(c)
 The most e
fective way of shuting down a runaway diesel engine is with an automatic air intake 
shutof valve; and
 
(d)
 4. Automatic over sped air intake shutof valves with automatic reset and ATEX
- aproval for 
Zone 1/2 hazardous area aplications shal be fited

A spark arestor is a device fited with the exhaust system of an engine to arest the glowing carbon 
particles and prevents the emision of flamable debris from combustion sources, such as internal 
combustion 
engines. The spark arestor shal have the folowing specifications:
 
 
(a)
 ATEX aproved for Zones 1 and/or 2 hazardous areas and designed for easy instalation
 
(b)
 Shal be designed to be used as part of an engine exhaust system during normal driving and operating
 
conditions. Design shal be of turbine type that works by traping through centrifugal force
 
(c)
 Shal be able to be mounted both horizontaly and verticaly
 
(d)
 10% Stainl
es Stel with minimum grade S
316
 
(e)
 Shal met the range of exhaust pipes of ADNOC and Group Companies flet of vehicles. End pipes 
shal be compatible with 
standards
 exhaust pipe sizes/clamps
 
(f)
 
Noise atenuation shal be 15 dB(A) typical
 
(g)
 Robust, non
- blocking and maintenance fre
 
(h)
 Shal met th
e spark aresting performance specified for diesel engine exhaust spark arestors for 
use in zone 1 and/or zone 2 hazardous areas in acordance to BS EN 1834
- 1/2/3:20
 
(i)
 
Shal be tested in acordance with European Directive 94/9/EC (the ATEX Directive); and
 
(j)
 
Waranty shal cover minimum 3 years for the entire spark arestor which includes structural or 
mechanical defects due to faulty materials or workmanship.
 
 
Zone A
 
 
Areas within the boundaries of the plant which are designated as Zone 1 or Zone 2 (Gas / 
Vapour) and Zone 
21 or Zone 2 (Dust) Hazardous Clasified Areas shal be designated as Zone A.
 The folowing requirements 
shal aply for vehicles entering the Zone A:
 
 
Diesel
- powered
 
 
Spark ignition engine vehicles (e.g. petrol or gasoline, LPG, CNG) are
 prohibited, as they are more likely to 
ignite a flamable gas cloud compared to a diesel
- powered vehicle from one of several sources including:
 
(a)
 Sparks from the high
- tension ignition system which is not designed to work in a flamable atmosphere
.  
(b)
 Hot 
catalytic converter which can reach temperatures up to 750oC; and
 
(c)
 Hot turbocharger (depending on how hard the car was recently driven).
 
ATEX
- aproved and fuly
- compliant with EN 1834
- 1 (Gas / Vapour) / EN 1834
- 3 (Dust) and EN 175 latest 
version.
 
Note: Veh
icles for use in potential flamable gas/vapour atmospheres including combustible dust shal fulfil 
the requirements of EN 1834
- 3 in adition to EN 1834
- 1.

on, and safety equipment to 
be used

ions including comercial aspects (where required the Manual of

Contractors shal ensure control measures, such as using apropriate ventilation and safety devices for the

Co
ntractors shal consider the use of habitats for Welding/Cuting activities in Hazardous clasified areas. 
Welding habitats are designed to provide a safe and controled work area where naked flame or hot work 
activities such as welding, grinding, cuting,
 can be caried out in clasified hazardous areas without the ned 
for a plant/production shutdown. 
In hazardous areas, based on the risk asesment, presurized habitats must 
be used where aplicable in line with ADNOC Group requirements.
 
Welding habitats
 can be built into various shapes and around any facility/plant obstacle, to provide the required 
protection, while constantly suplied with presurization/breathable air to prevent the ingres of explosive 
gases or vapours, which are likely to be present.
 
Containment of welding habitat enclosures shal be aranged in a way that maintain the prevention of welding 
slag and welding/grinding sparks from escaping to the posible explosive environment. 
 
Only presurized habitats systems that are third party cert
ified to standards IEC6079
- 13 shal be operated 
within Zone 1 & 2 hazardous areas.
 
Equipment and personel involved in the hot works shal be certified and aproved as per ADNOC Group 
requirements.
 
Contractors shal ensure that a Fire watch is posted wher
e there is a potential for fire or whenever any of the 
folowing conditions ocur during the activity: 
 
° 
Proximity of combustible closer than radius of 35 fet (10.7 meters) to the point of activity
   
° 
Openings within a radius of 35 fet (10.7 meters) which 
expose combustible material in adjacent area, 
including concealed spaces in wals, ceilings or flors
   
° 
Proximity to hydrocarbon lines and equipment; and 
 
° 
Combustible material adjacent to the oposite side of metal partitions, wals, ceiling, or rofs, or 
in 
contact with pipes, and are likely to be ignited by conduction or radiation
 
Contractors shal ensure that any

and ventilated, or purged with an inert gas, and certified as 'gas fre'

be performed on it.
 
Contractors shal ensure that Fire blankets and curtains are required to be listed, aproved, or the equivalent 
for such use. As a minimum these shal comply with ANSI/FM 4950 [Ref. 3] or equivale
nt. 
 
Contractors shal ensure that fire protection, fire extinguishers and trained fire watch are provided at the work 
site based on the risk asesment, continuously monitors worksite conditions during job progres for changes 
that could compromise the sa
fety and ensure that apropriate warning signage is in place at the relevant 
worksite locations to inform of the danger and/or aces restrictions.
 
Fire watch shal be wel trained in the use of diferent types of fire extinguishers.

responsibilities

USE OF COMPRESED GAS CYLINDERS
 
 
Labeling and 
Colour
 Coding
 
 
(i) Label
 
 
Each individual gas cylinder shal have a label which identifies its contents, it also provides basic safety 
information on the hazard(s) asociated with the product. The label is the primary method of identifying the 
contents. It is the responsibility of 
the person filing the gas cylinder to afix a label. The labeling of the 
compresed gas cylinder shal be in line with BS EN 1089
- 3, Transportable Gas Cylinders. Gas cylinder 
identification (excluding LPG), 
Colour
 coding.
 
 
(i) 
Colour
 Codes
 
 
Colour
 codin
g shal be aplied to the shoulder, or curved part, at the top of the cylinder. 
Colours
 are chosen 
based on the properties of content in acordance with the hazard of the gas filed. Some specific gases and 
gas mixtures are asigned 
colours
 relevant to the
 gas or gas mixture. Instead of the hazard 
colour
, other 
colours
 may be asigned based on the components of a gas mixture.
 
 
Colour
 coding for Specific Single Gases
-  The folowing gases shal be identified by specific 
colours
 rather 
than the 
colour
 system defined above.
 
 
° 
Flamable gases. For example 
-  Acetylene: Maron
 
° 
Oxidizing gases. For example 
-  Oxygen: White, Nitrous oxide: Blue
 
° 
Inert gases. For example 
-  Argon: Dark Gren, Nitrogen: Black
 
Contractor shal ensure that the storage areas for compresed gas cylinders shal be col dry, wel ventilated

udible alarms both inside and outside the storage 
area
.

from fuel gas cylinders by at least 6.1 m (20 
ft.
), or by a wal at least 1.5 m (5 
ft.
) high
 with a minimum half
-
hour fire resistance
.

self
-  contained breathing underwater breathing aparatus in adition to the ful retest period of 5
 years, an 
internal visual inspection neds to be performed every 2, 5 years.

WORKING AT HEIGHT
 /SCAFOLDING
 
Contractors shal refer to ADNOC HSE Standards, 
HSE
- OS
- ST16 and HSE
- OS
- ST2 for details and 
necesary compliance.
 
 
Minimum Requirements
 
 
Contractors shal conduct working at height activities and scafolding instalations by utilizing only competent 
resources, materials compliant with ADNOC HSE 
Standard
s  specifications /
ADNOC 
Gr
oup requirements.
 
Any work being performed at height equal to or greater than 1.8 meters is considered as working at height 
acordingly, extra controls (e.g. Body Harnes) shal be placed.
 
Contractors shal deploy trained and cert
ified personel to erect, modify and dismantle scafolding e.g. 
scafolding erector, scafolding supervisor and scafolding inspector. The identified roles must be independent 
from one another
.  
Scafolding systems must be designed by a competent person and must be erected, modified and dismantled 
as per ADNOC 
HSE 
Standards
 specifications
.   
Scafolding boards and laders shal be of iron preferably or fire retardant material in the proces areas. 
Contractor shal be required to inspect each scafold with a competent person before and after any 
modifications, and after any event that may have afected the scafold structure
.  
Scaf
- tag system shal be implemented for inspection to display the status 
of scafolding
.  
Wherever rope aces is utilized for the activities, Contractor shal folow the ADNOC HSE 
Standards
 / 
ADNOC 
Group relevant requirements
.  
Work at height with personel basket shal be strictly limited (short time work or inspection only). I
n these 
cases, the basket and lifting apliances such as cranes shal be of a certified type
.  
Contractors shal utilize fal protection systems as per ADNOC HSE 
Standards, 
ADNOC 
Group working
 at 
height /scafolding procedures, which includes working over w
ater where aplicable
.  
Contractors shal provide adequate number of rescue equipment for working at height
.  
Contractors shal provide and maintain a safety net system to prevent fal from height (personal/material) 
wherever required e.g. where there is pub
lic interference and distance betwen adjacent buildings is minimal
.  
Working at Height means work in any place where, if precautions were not taken, a person could fal a distance 
liable to cause personal injury. Working at height includes, but not limited
 to, working:
 
-  
On a flat rof;
 
-  
On fragile surfaces;
 
-  
On scafoldings;
 
-  
Using aces equipment (such as, laders, gangways, cradle, man
- basket); and
 
-  
At ground level close to holes (e.g. excavations) edges or ledges (e.g. retaining wals).
 
Extra controls (e.g. 
Body Harnes) shal be placed when working at height is greater or equal to 1.8 meters. 
Working at height does not include a slip or a trip on the level, as a fal from height has to involve a fal from 
one level to a lower level, nor does it include walki
ng up and down a permanent staircase in a building. 
Therefore, it is esential to strictly establish and implement a fal protection strategy while planing to working 
at height
.  
Working at height shal be avoided wherever posible. If not, then asociated
 hazards shal be identified and 
control measures defined during the mandated Job Safety Analysis (JSA).
 
ADNOC Group shal ensure personel required for working at height shal be selected as per their ability to 
perform work. 
 
Contractor
s  shal submit Med
ical fitnes certificate 
for personel asigned to working at height. 
 
ADNOC 
HSE 
Standards
 shal be refered for training and competency for al works at height.

equipment

uipment that is used at or around worksite

ntains personal training and competency records.

pre use inspection check list for laders, scafolds and fal protection systems on a

SUSPENSION OF
 WORKING
 AT HEIGHT
 
 
If fal 
elimination is not posible, the folowing aproaches shal be folowed/ demonstrated for working at 
height: 
 
 
° 
Fal Prevention
 
° 
Fal Protection
 
° 
Fal Aresting
 
 
Suspension of working at height shal be considered when the wind sped reaches or gusts higher than 38 
km/hr or 20.5 knots and then ensure review of the work based on risk asesment considering the curent and 
anticipated wind sped, weather conditions, 
altitude of work being undertaken and any other conditions 
(hazards) that may afect the safety and health of personel working at height.
    
 
WORKING PLATFORM 
 
 
The working platform shal conform to the folowing: 
 
 
° 
Suficient strength and rigidity for the intended use; 
 
° 
Erected under supervision of scafolding supervisor/inspector and taged after completion; 
 
° 
Used in a maner that prevents acidental displacement; 
 
° 
Remains stable at al times, including during and a
fter modification; 
 
° 
Have suficient dimensions to permit fre pasage of persons and safe use of equipment
  and 
materials; 
 
° 
At least of 60m wide; 
 
° 
Constructed so that the surface has no gap likely to cause injury or through which an object could fal 
on 
to any person below; and 
 
° 
Maintained to prevent slips or trips and be provided with guardrails, toe boards, handholds and 
fotholds 
 
 
MOBILE ELEVATED WORK PLATFORMS (MEWP) 
 
 
° 
MEWP
 is a mechanical device which includes scisor lift, aerial platform, or an extensible or 
articulating bom aerial device (either self
- propeled or vehicle
- mounted) used to provide temporary 
aces for people or equipment to inacesible areas, usualy at 
height 
 
 
° 
They are generaly used for temporary, flexible aces purposes such as maintenance and 
construction work or by fire fighters for emergency aces, which distinguishes them from permanent 
aces equipment such as elevators 
 
 
The folowing 
shal
 be
 confirmed:
 
 
° 
Operators of MEWPs shal be trained by authorized party and competent to use the equipment
 
° 
The training given shal be specific to the type of equipment the person is operating and shal be in 
acordance with internationaly recognized 
training, such as The International Powered Aces 
Federation
- Powered Aces License (PAL) or equivalent;
 
° 
MEWP shal be examined at six monthly intervals or major repair whichever is earlier, by authorized 
person. Copies of such certification shal be kept
 for record purpose by the equipment custodian;

irements, limitations and work suspension conditions as stipulated in the

sion hazard to be complied with

Rope aces techniques alow aces to structures or equipment that are otherwise inacesible,

t Authority (SPA)

posts, but not

Barge Superintendent/ Barge Captain/ Rig Manager/ Vesel Master.

Service Life of Lifting Equipment
 
 
Manufacturer shall provide the Owner the expected lif
available, folowing criteria shal be folowed:
 
 
° 
For Pedestal Cranes, Overhead Cranes and Jib Cranes: 25 years from the Date of put
- into
- service 
for
 the first time. 
 
° 
For Mobile Cranes: 25 years from the Date of put
- into
- service for the first time (25 years of life span 
specified in latest regulation of by Abu Dhabi Trafic Department). 
 
° 
For Tower Cranes and Forklifts: 20 years from the Date of put
- into
- service for the first time. 
 
 
Condition Assessment shall be carried out by the Crane
to renew the anual Mobile Crane License and in 
order to determine the extension of life span.

o be

d for al Lifting related activities shal be managed in compliance with

EXCAVATION
 
Contractors shal refer to 
ADNOC HSE Standard, HSE
- OS
- ST15 for details and necesary compliance.
 
Minimum Requirements
 
Contractors shal conduct excavation activities by utilizing competent resources and implement necesary 
precautions.
 
Mechanical excavations shal not be used in the
 proximity of service equipment or facility as per the distance 
specified in the 
ADNOC 
Group procedures. Al excavation equipment shal be inspected and aproved by 
ADNOC Group representatives
.  
Any excavation shal be adequately protected by shoring/slopin
g/benching if people are required to work in 
the excavation and the depth is more than 1.2 m. Shoring must be designed by a qualified person
.   
Contractors shal implement 
ADNOC 
Group permit to work system for excavations. Confined Space 
permit/certificate 
shal be obtained in line with 
ADNOC 
Group procedures when the excavation depth exceds 
the limit set by the 
ADNOC 
Group. 
Any work in excavation after a severe weather condition like rain, heavy 
wind etc., shal be permited only after an inspection by a 
competent person
.  
Contractors shal implement the requirements of ADNOC
 Group procedures and safe work instructions for 
Trenches and Excavations
.  
Excavations shal be properly marked with proper signs and baricades and means of escape and rescue must 
be 
identified during design
.   
Proper aces into excavations one meter dep or more such as laders or stairs shal be provided. 
Crosovers and aces to be provided at stipulated intervals along the entire length of the excavation as 
aplicable
.  
Existing und
erground instalations shal be marked on site prior to any excavation. Efort shal be made to 
determine if there are existing underground facilities/ instalations
.  
Contractors
 shal ensure that al Formwork suports are constructed to ADNOC
 G roup aprov
ed 
standards
.  
The total working area shal be checked on underground service plans and where required shal be scaned 
using an ADNOC Group aproved Cable Avoidance Tol (CAT) to ascertain the presence of electrical cables 
and underground services. Before 
excavation work is caried out, it shal ensure a competent person aseses 
the work requirements for the site.
 
 
Only Operators who have an authorized driving license and have ben confirmed competent shal be alowed 
to operate earthmoving equipment; and 
 
 
Excavation equipment shal always be guided by banks man/flag man who shal be aware of the movement 
an
d operation of heavy equipment.
 
Where the excavation is to be greater than 1 metre width, scaning shal be caried out at both sides of the 
proposed e
xcavation and at every 5 metres acros the trench. 
 
 
IDENTIFICATION OF UNDERGROUND SERVICES & OVERHEAD 
POWER LINE
 
 
Site survey shal be conducted to identify the presence of underground service and overhead power lines as 
part of planing.

spaces and the rules around conf
ined space entry aply

cted with anti

pe, size or weight of an article canot be easily moved or fit into a spray both

°

and also operate a minimum of a 5 minute

Ventilation System
  
 
The purpose of ventilation is to: 
 
° 
Draw overspray away from the personel
 
° 
Control flamable and hazardous 
vapours
 
° 
Colect 
vapours
, droplets and solid part
icles; and 
 
° 
Filter or wash the air before it is discharged. 
 
The ventilation shal be suficient to prevent the formation of dangerous concentrations of flamable 
vapours
. 
As a rule, the maximum flamable concentration in a spray both or spray space durin
g normal operation 
shal not exced 25% of the Lower Explosive Limit (LEL)

reasonably practicable, have at least six metres horizontal and two metres vertical clearance above and below 
the place where the paint is being aplied. 
 
 
SPRAYING IN CONFINED SPACES
  
 
Spraying is often used to aply coatings to the inside of storage tanks, vesels and other enclosed roms and 
spaces. In an unventilated enclosure, a harmful 
vapour
 concentration may be formed within a few seconds of 
starting spraying, and a flamable atmo
sphere within a few minutes. 
 
 
ADNOC Confined Space 
Standard
 shal be folowed for working under these conditions. As a minimum

°

rts of the 
confined space is at a safe level. The maximum concentration of flamable 
vapour
 shal be identified 
in the permit in line with the requirements of ADNOC Permit to Work 
Standards
;

vapour
 concen
tration above the specified limit is 
detected imediately since personel wearing breathing aparatus and protective clothing may not 
be aware of increasing levels of 
vapour
; 
 
 
° 
Vapours
 from spraying are likely to acumulate at flor level so extraction at
 this level is esential; 
 
 
° 
The discharge point shal be situated in a safe place away from any building, work area or source of 
ignition;

refore in a safe position. In this event, if conductors are likely to come within reach ocasionaly,

WORKING IN POTENTIALY FLAMABLE ATMOSPHERE
 
 
Electrical and non
- electrical equipmen
t and instalations in potentialy explosive atmospheres shal be specialy 
designed and constructed so that the risks of ignition are eliminated or reduced. Techniques to do this include 
sealing electrical equipment so that the explosive atmosphere canot

components, reducing the power of electrical equipment, and de

or an explosive atmosphere is detected. 
 
 
Controling potential sources of ignition in a hazardous area may be achieved by: 
 
° 
Use of suitably
- rated electrical equipment (e.g. intrinsicaly safe or flame
- prof)
 
° 
Ensuring electrical equipment is efectively maintained where porly maintained electrica
l equipment

°

°

chemicals may ignite spontaneo
usly above certain temperatures; 
 
°

welding).

ated voltage of 10V (in the case of portable

held portable electrical equipment should be suplied at 10V ac maximum (± 5 V act to earth)

Group procedures and safe work instructions while handling

ealth and safety information such as Safety

ork

aws and regulations.

ained in the folowing and be under the direct supervision of a person with the folowing

STORAGE OF EXPLOSIVES
 
 
Explosives are very delicate and environmental factors such as temperature and humidity have an impact on 
their performance and stability. Explosives shall be 
recomendations. 
 
General requirements of ex
plosives storage shal include, but are not limited to: 
 
° 
Never keping primed explosives in storage. 
 
° 
Only temporary storage shal be alowed at operating sites for the specific operations required. 
 
° 
Dedicated storage that is at a safe distance from any ha
zards. 
 
° 
Detonators and explosives shal be stored separately and a minimum distance of 25 meters shal be 
kept betwen them. 
 
° 
Storing only in their original packaging.
 
° 
Keping away from exposure to direct sunlight. 
 
° 
Properly stacking and securing from any acidental impact. 
 
 
TRANSPORTATION OF EXPLOSIVES 
 
 
Any transport of explosives shal comply with UAE government regulations and Police requirements dictating 
time and quantities alowed. 
 
A journey management plan s
hal be in place before the start of the journey. The plan has to include, but wil 
not be limited to: 
 
° 
Selection of safest and most secure route avoiding populated areas and industrial areas 
 
° 
Safe rest stops 
 
° 
Security escort (prefered with uniformed national law enforcement guard) 
 
° 
Emergency response protocol in case of vehicle break down 
 
The vehicle used to transport the explosives shal be fit
- for
- purpose and wel
- maintained. The vehicle shal be 
equiped w
ith emergency equipment such as a first aid kit and fire extinguishers. 
 
Non
- sparking material shal cover any parts of the vehicle which may come in contact with the explosive load. 
T he vehicle shal have at least one fire extinguisher
 in the vehicle along with a first aid box. 
There shal be no radio equipment fixed inside the vehicle, unles they come equiped with aproved 
magazines with detonator segregation. 
 
The vehicle shal
 be protected with at least one security guard. It can never be left unatended.
 
Vehicles carying explosives must not stop to refuel or enter a workshop for services. 
 
Explosives and detonators shal be transported in separate Faraday boxes, preferably pl
aced in separate 
vehicles altogether. 
 
Only authorized pasengers trained in handling explosives may travel with together with the explosives. 
 
Al the stored hazardous substances shal have hazard warning labels on the storage container. The purpose 
of ha
zard warning labels is to imediately alert personel at a facility that hazardous substance in the storage 
area have the potential to cause harm to human health and the environment. 
 
These labels shal comply with the folowing requirements: 
 
  
° 
Al Hazar
dous material container and package shal be clearly identified and labeled, regardles of 
its size or whether it is a temporary or permanent container;
 
° 
Al Hazardous substances stored in a storage container shal have suficient space avail
able for a 
hazard warning label

- label it using an extra copy suplied by the

uct identifier. Al text shal be 
provided in

ROAD TRANSPORT 
 
 
The folowing requirements shal be aplied for the transportation of hazardous substances by road: 
 
  
° 
Vehicles used to cary drums are drop
- sided;
 
° 
In adition to the vehicl
e fire extinguisher, an extinguisher apropriate to the load is caried; 
 
° 
In adition to basic PE requirements, drivers and their helpers are isued with PE apropriate to 
the load being caried, e.g. gogles, chemical handling gloves, etc. 
 
 
Tanker 
vehicles
 carying flamable / hazardous substances shal have: 
 
 
° 
An exhaust system forward of the front axle, below
directed away from the tank on the driver's side of the vehicle or an exhaust system fited
 with an 
ATEX aproved flame/spark arestor;
 
° 
Anti
- static tyres i.e. conductive, with a maximum resistance of 1 x 106 Ohms;
 
° 
A master isolation switch outside the cab to isolate al electrical circuits, except the tachograph/ IVMS. 
The switch shal be clear
ly marked in Arabic and English "Electrical Isolation Switch" in red leters on 
a white background. The "On" and "Of" positions of the switch itself shal be clearly marked in Arabic 
and English. A sign in Arabic and English reading "In Case of an Acide
nt or Fire Put this Switch to 
Of" shal be fited next to the switch. The sign shal be red text on a white background, readable at a 
distance of 5m;
 
° 
Batery terminals that are covered to prevent acidental shorting;
 
° 
The loading and discharge piping / va
lving shal be designed in such a way that when in transit, the 
flamable substance is only caried within the body of the tank, not within the piping external to the 
tank;
 
° 
Electrical wiring protected by solid draw tubing suitable for bulk vehicles caryin
g Clas 1 petroleum 
products;
 
° 
A fire
- resistant shield, screning the engine from the body of the vehicle if the engine protrudes behind 
the rear of the cab, or if the engine is open to the rear. The fire scren shal cover any unenclosed 
part of the 
engine above or to the rear of the cab and extend down to the top of the chasis side 
members;
 
° 
At least one means of earthing the tanker for use during loading and unloading operations;
 
° 
A vacum relief valve, with suitable flame/spark arestor, which may b
e fited within the manhole; 
 
° 
Drivers shal be equiped with al necesary Safe Handling Of Chemicals (SHOC) and Transport 
Emergency Cards (TREM) acording to the vehicle load;
 
° 
Shal  be  fited  with  rolover protection device which automaticaly shu
ts down the engine and 
isolates the batery. This device shal not get activated at les than 45° to the vertical; 
 
° 
The vent and dumper valves shal be suitable for the apropriate charge/discharge flow rate with 
safety relief for presure/vacum venting w
ith integral flame trap, mounted on the manhole cover;
 
° 
Overfil protection and vapour recovery system shal be instaled;

SEA TRANSPORT 
 
 
The transportation of hazardous substances by sea, and storage on board a vesel, is caried 
out in 
acordance with the International Maritime Dangerous Gods (IMDG) Code. The Code lays down basic 
principles; detailed recomendations for individual substances, materials and articles, and a number of 
recomendations for god operational practice in
cluding advice on terminology, packing, labeling, stowage, 
segregation and handling, and emergency response action. 
 
  
In adition, the folowing requirements aply:
 
 
° 
Drums of chemicals are transported in drum cages specialy designed and constructed for 
that 
purpose; 
 
° 
Cargoes of paletized chemicals remain straped to the palets; 
 
° 
Damaged palets are not used and are replaced before lifting is atempted; 
 
° 
Special palet lifting devices are used to lift palets from the quayside onto the vesel, and from
 the 
vesel to the platform/rig barge; 
 
° 
Al other packaged chemicals are contained in transport or cargo boxes; and 
 
° 
Only lifting equipment with cure
nt, valid certification is used
  
° 
If 
standards
 ISO containers or other types of certified lifting aplianc
e are used for the transportation 
of hazardous substances, the dispatcher shal ensure that suply labels are conspicuously duplicated 
on the dor of the container or on the side of the lifting aplian
ce
  
  
AIR TRANSPORT 
 
 
Hazardous substances shal only b
e transported by air in emergencies. In such cases, advice wil be given by 
the Chief Pilot who decides if the hazardous substances can be transported in acordance with the 
International Civil Aviation Organization Technical Instructions for the Safe Tran
sport of Dangerous Gods by 
Air.

REQUIREMENT FOR PROPER STORAGE OF HAZARDOUS MATERIALS
 
 
° 
In a storage area, hazardous materials having the most risk for causing serious harm shal be 
segregated (or separated) from the others. The segregation of substances shal be ensured based on 
the hazardous materials compatibility charts; 
 
° 
The hazardous materials storage area shal only be dedicated to storing hazardous substances. 
Stored materials shal be properly stacked to minimize the risk of being easily knocked down; 
 
° 
Storage of hazardous materials in open storage area can be acepted
 for some materials under the 
condition that such storage conditions does not contradict with the storage conditions specified in the 
SDS. Open storage areas shal be secured, and al stored materials shal be covered with flame
-
retardant covers; 
 
° 
Stored m
aterials that can be damaged by exposure to water shal be kept a minimum of 3 inches 
above the flor (such as on palets);
 
° 
Hazardous materials shal not be stored in alphabetical order because this could result in the storage 
of incompatible materials nea
r each other; 
 
° 
If a hazardous material has more than one hazard clas, then priority must be given to the highest 
hazard clas (primary hazard); 
 
° 
Hazardous materials shal be stored in stacks for easy aces; 
 
° 
Stack locations shal be clearly identified wi
th signs; 
 
° 
The height of the stacks shal not be from the flor up to the ceiling. Instead, there should be a 
minimum of 3 fet betwen the top of the stacks and the ceiling; 
 
° 
Stacks shal be aranged so they do not obstruct the movement of forklifts and o
ther equipment; 
 
° 
Empty containers and expired materials shal be stored in a separate, designated area, and the area 
must be labelled as —Quarantine Area“ or —Expired 
 
° 
Special outlets shal be located in the storage area that is conected t
o sumps, which colect the 
leaked or spiled hazardous material

ired respiratory equipment such as EBA, SCBA 
& Air lop systems 
as 
aplicable to the scope of the activities along with personal toxic detectors to al workers to ensure safe escape 
from the location in case of H2S emergency
.

sidered over SCBA for RED Zones

ecify the SCBA/air
- line requirements;
 
°

Personel, including temporary workers and visitors entering Red, Amber or Yelow zones shal be provided

° 
Alarm at the toxic gas warning level in such a way a
s to be recognizable under al foreseable 
conditions including in high noise areas 
 
° 
A system shal be in place to ensure that personal H2S detectors are properly maintained 
 
° 
Devices shal be tested and calibrated
 
WORKING IN RED, AMBER AND YELOW ZONES
 
 
Re
d Zones
 
 
Al personel entering a red zone shal have H2S training, competence and certification, cary a personal H2S 
monitor and don breathing aparatus and use it at al times.
 
 
As a minimum, the folowing shal be considered:
 
 
° 
Aces control shal be 
adhered to as mentioned in Section 7.2.5
 of the standard
 
° 
At al times SCBA set shal be doned and in use and for al prolonged work use airline fed breathing 
aparatus shal be ensured
 
° 
Provide a means of constant comunication (i.e. fixed plant telephone 
or hand held radio) with 
someone located outside the Red Zone, in adition to use of the budy system;
 
° 
Move any operator roms/shelter, e.g. those for sour gas compresors, outside the red zones, or 
make the operator rom a temporary refuge;
 
° 
Avoid as far a
s posible any work such as painting, or civil works maintenance until the proces plant 
is depresurized. This is reasonably practical if the plant is designed for this aproach, e.g. has god 
segregation distances and is designed for minimum onsite work;
 
° 
Avoid as far as posible the ned for routine work in the Red Zone for example: use transmiters rather 
than gauges, and automatic sampling rather than manual sample taking; and 
 
° 
Operating companies shal review whether it is necesary to cary out the ta
sks in the Red Zone
 while 
the plant is operating. The work activities in the RED Zone shal be minimize to absolute minimum as 
far as practicable
 
 
Amber Zones
 
 
Al personel entering an Amber zone shal have H2S training, competence and certification, 
shal wear a 
personal H2S monitor, and, as a minimum, shal cary an Emergency Escape Breathing Aparatus (EBA) or 
have it at hand‘s reach.
 
For those, involved in performing activities requiring opening of proces components which contain, or may 
contain 
H2S (intrusive work/breaking containment) shal have the breathing airline aparatus or SCBA set 
doned and in use;

As a minimum the folowing shal be considered:
 
 
° 
Aces control shal be adhered to as mentioned in Section 7.2.5
 of the standard
 
° 
Provide a 
means of constant comunication (i.e. fixed plant telephone or hand held radio) with 
someone located outside the Amber Zone, in adition to use of the budy system;
 
° 
Moving any operator roms/shelters, e.g. those for sour gas compresors, outside the Amber 
zone, 
or making it a temporary refuge; and
 
° 
Avoid as far as posible any work such as painting, civil works maintenance until the proces plant is 
depresurized. This is reasonably practical if the plant is designed for this aproach, e.g. has god 
segregat
ion distances and is designed for minimum onsite work.
 
 
Yelow Zones
 
 
Anyone entering a Yelow Zone shal have H2S training, competence and certification, be aware of the location 
of strategicaly placed EBA sets and shal wear a H2S Monitor.
 
While the risk of exposure to dangerous concentrations of H2S is much lower when working on equipment in 
the yelow zone, there can stil be a significant risk of toxic injury and ilnes during some operations. Opening 
of H2S containing equipment shal on
ly be undertaken by persons doning breathing airline aparatus or 
SCBA set and a portable H2S detector used for monitoring.
 
For sampling activity in H2S Zones, SCBA shal be worn and the area be cleared to a safe distance while 
opening and isolating and u
ntil the equipment is purged and while de
- spading and restoring to service. The 
area shal be marked of and signed as for Red and Amber Zones during these activities. The size of the 
restricted area shal be determined during the Job Safety Asesment (JS
A) based on dispersion analysis.
 
 
MONITORING EQUIPMENT
- MAINTENANCE, TESTING AND CALIBRATION
 
 
The equipment used for monitoring the exposure levels of H2S shal be serviced and tested at intervals 
recomended by the manufacturer, and posibly more 
frequently under extreme humidity, temperature, dust 
or other adverse environmental operating conditions.
 
Status calibration shal be checked before every use of portable gas monitors in acordance with the 
manufacturer's recomendation. Records of device 
testing and maintenance shal be kept for verification and 
audit purposes.
 
 
Bump Testing
 
 
Bump testing is the only way to ensure proper sensor and alarm functionality. A bump test is defined as the 
proces of briefly exposing sensors in a gas detector to a
n expected concentration of gas that is greater than 
the alarm set points. The purpose of the bump test is to check for sensor and alarm functionality. However, it 
does not check for acuracy. It is important to note that acuracy is ensured through calibr
ation, which is a 
completely diferent proces than bump testing.
 
The bump test for personal gas detectors shall be do
recomendation whichever comes earlier. However, for portable gas monitors used by Autho
rized Gas Tester 
shal be bump tested before every use. Records for the bump testing shal be maintained. Any gas monitor 
that fails to respond properly during a functional bump test shal be sent for troubleshoting or further 
maintenance as required.
 
 
Se
lf - Testing
 
 
The personal detectors shal be self
- tested each day before the use of the detector. The device wil perform a 
self
- test by operating the buzzer, LED‘s, vibrator and LC
the display.

Cali
bration
 
 
Calibration is a quantitative test utilizing a known traceable concentration of test gas to demonstrate that the 
sensor(s) and alarms respond to the gas within manufa
baseline and sensitivity of the se
nsor/electronics circuit, that is, it is based on an exposure to a known gas in 
which the sensor/instrument is adjusted to that concentration. Calibrations are required on a periodic basis. 
 
Al sensors regardles of the operational principle, wil drift o
ver a period of time and require adjustments. 
Sensors may also experience drift after an over
- exposure to the targeted gas, or after exposure to extreme 
environmental conditions, after a severe physical jolt, or if a Function Test produces a les than sati
sfactory 
result. It is important to observe the response times of the sensors, the stability of the sensor signal, and the 
recovery times of the sensors during this period. 
 
The calibration interval for each of these sensors from the diferent makers of th
e instruments may be diferent. 
The frequency of calibration shall be governed as per 
instruction manual for the routine test and re
- calibration interval.
 
 
TRAINING, COMPETENCE & CERTIFICATION
 
 
Training
 
 
Training is crucial for enabling people to acquire the necesary skils and knowledge that ensure they are 
capable to safely undertake tasks that may expose them to the hazards of H2S. The key to efective training 
is to understand the specific job 
requirements and individual abilities.
 
H2S training shal therefore be provided to al personel includes specific potential exposures and include, as 
aplicable, awarenes training, formal clasrom instruction, in adition to on the job training and coac
hing. 
 
 
Competence
 
 
Personel having potential exposure to H2S, shal be asesed and declared competent against aplicable 
industry acepted performance 
standards
 such as OPITO Basic H2S Training or A
NSI/ASP Z390.1 as 
aplicable).
 
Demonstrating required 
level of Competence for working in hazardous conditions shal involve aplication of 
technical training, demonstration specific skil.
 
 
Certification
 
 
Documented evidence shal be provided/required in the form of a Certificate from aproved training 
centre
/awarding body to al personel on sucesful completion of H2S training.
 
 
MINIMUM TRAINING FOR AL PERSONEL

shal as a minimum, be t
rained as per the folowing:

zones;
 
° 
Use of personal H2S detectors;
 
° 
Proper use and maintenance of RPE for working in H2S atmospheres, usage instruction and hands
-
on practice;

MARINE CREW REQUIREMENTS 
 
ACEPTANCE CRITERIA FOR MARINE CONTRACTORS 
 
Prior to the start of any contract, ADNOC Group shal ensure 
that 
the Contractor is 
required to have as a 
minimum an experienced and qualified Port Captain (or Marine Superintendent), Port Enginer (or Enginer 
Superintendent) and HSE Manager/Enginer/Oficer within its UAE shore management suport structure. 
Aditional suport may be req
uired depending on the number of vesels and the nature of the work conducted 
by the contractor. 
 
The minimum certification requirement for these Contractors shal be in line to the requirements of Standards 
of Training, Certification and Watch keping for
 Seafarers (STCW) [Ref. 1] as aplicable. It establishes basic 
requirements on training, certification and watch
- keping on an internationaly consistent basis. 
 
ADNOC Group shal ensure that al personel asociated to marine operations shal undergo the
 requirements 
and be certified as mentioned in STCW, as aplicable. 
 
The CVs of Contractor's senior marine personel shal be reviewed and aproved before engagement of any 
vesel on contract under ADNOC Group control, and in acordance with the relevant "
Marine Crew 
Familiarization Policies & Procedures".

responsibilities shal be establishe
d .

and shal be subject to verification by the ADNOC Group
.

.  
Marine 
crew competence shal as a minimum be in acordance with the requirements stated in STCW or 
equivalent
.  
Persons undergoing training shall not be considered a
minimum safe maning levels.
 
Contractor 
Competence Requirements

Enginer (or Enginer Superintendent) and HSE Manager/Enginer/Oficer within their shore management 
structure. 
 
The qualifica
tions and minimum competence requirements for these persons shal be detailed within the 
Contractor Company‘s HSE Management System.  In tur
apropriately qualified and experienced marine personel are apointed to
 vesels under their responsibility. 
 
The Curiculum Vitae of the contractor's senior marine personel shal be reviewed and aproved by ADNOC 
Group before engagement of any vesel on contract. 
 
Safety training of Contractor's personel shal only be rec
ognized if conducted by organizations or individuals 
that have ben aproved by ADNOC Group. Contractors shal be required to kep records to demonstrate 
that such safety training has ben caried out as required.

DIVING CONTRACTOR
 
On any diving project/operation, there neds to be one company in overal control of the diving operations. 
This wil normaly be the company who employs the divers. If there is more than one company employ
ing 
divers then there shal be a writen agrement as to which of these companies is in overal control.  The 
company in control is caled the Diving Contractor. 
 
The name of the Diving Contractor shal be clearly displayed. Al personel, clients and oth
ers involved in the 
diving operation shal be aware who the Diving Contractor is. 
 
The Diving Contractor shal provide a defined management structure in writing.  This shal include 
arangements for a clear hand over of supervisory responsibilities at apr
opriate stages in the operation, again 
recorded in writing. 
 
The Diving Contractor‘s responsibilities shall include
 
° 
Risk asesments have ben caried out both onshore and at site. 
 
 
° 
The place from which operations are to be car
ried out is suitable and safe. 
 
 
° 
There are suficient personel of the required competency in the diving team. 
 
 
° 
The personel are qualified and competent. Divers shal be medicaly qualified for using breathing 
aparatus/equipment for diving and certified by acredited institute for diving operation. 
 
 
° 
The plant and equipment is corectly certified and properly mai
ntained. 
 
° 
Before any diving is caried out there should be an aproved Diving Project Plan in existence. The 
aproved Diving Project Plan should consist of aproved project specific documents. [IMCA D 014; 
Ref. 40, Section 7.1 —Diving Project Plan“.] 
 
 
° 
S uitable site
- specific safety and familiarisation training is provided to al members of the dive and 
suport teams. 
 
 
° 
Adequate arangements exist for first aid and medical treatment of personel. 
 
° 
Emergency response and contingency plan to be updated and i
n place. 
 
° 
Contractor
s  should have a Diving Management System (DMS) in place and implemented. 
 
° 
Apropriate insurance policies which shal include third party liability and apropriate insurance cover 
al divers. 
 
° 
There is a clear reporting and responsibilit
y structure laid out in writing.

DIVING SUPERVISOR
 
The training and competence requirements for a Diving supervisor are detailed in IMCA
 C 03 Rev 3.1, 
February 2017: —Guidance on Competence Assurance and A
 
Diving Supervisors are responsible for the operation that they have ben apointed to supervise, and they 
shal only hand over control to another supervisor apointed in wri
ting by the Diving Contractor.  Such a 
handover shal be entered in the relevant operations logbok. 
 
Diving Supervisors can only supervise as much of a diving operation as they can personaly control both during 
routine operations and if an emergency shou
ld ocur. 
 
The Diving Supervisor with overal responsibility for the operation is the only person who can order the start 
of a dive, subject to apropriate work permits etc. Other relevant parties, such as the relevant Area Authority 
(as defined in the Wo
rk Management System
)  can, however, tel the Diving Supervisor to terminate a dive for 
safety or operational reasons. 
 
There wil be times, for example, during operations from a vesel using dynamic positioning (DP) techniques 
that the Diving Supervisor wi
l ned to liaise closely with other personel, such as the vesel Master or the DP 
operator.  In such circumstances, the Diving Superv
responsibility for the overal safety of the vesel and its ocupants. 
 
The Diving Supervisor is entitled to give direct orders in relation to health and safety to any person taking part 
in, or who has any influence over, the diving operation. These orders take precedence over any company 
hierarchy. These orders could inclu
de instructing unecesary personel to leave a control area, instructing 
personel to operate equipment, etc. This authority does not extend to the duties of the Master of the vesel. 
 
To ensure that the diving operation is caried out safely, Diving Sup
ervisors shal consider a number 
of points regarding the workforce including 
that:
  
They are competent to cary out such work, and that they understand their own areas and levels of 
responsibility and who is responsible for any other relevant areas. Such 
responsibilities shal be contained in 
the relevant documentation. They shal be in posesion of a leter from the Diving Contractor apointing 
them as a Diving Supervisor
.  
They are competent to cary out the work required of them.  This includes that 
personel are fit and in 
posesion of a valid medical certificate of fitnes
.  
Equipment they propose to use for any particular operation is adequate, safe, properly certified and maintained. 
They can do this by confirming that the equipment mets the req
uirements set down in this Standards. 
Equipment is adequately checked by themselves or another competent person prior to its use. Such checks 
shal be documented, for example, on a pre
- prepared checklist, and recorded in the diving operations log for 
the
 project
.  
When the operation uses, or plans to use, complex or potentialy hazardous equipment, they shal ensure that 
the posible hazards have ben evaluated and are fuly understod by al relevant parties and that, if required, 
training is given. This
 shal be caried out as part of the risk asesment during the planing of the operation 
and shal be documented.  If the situation changes, further risk asesment shal be considered.  Diving 
Supervisors shal met their responsibilities by ensuring the
 documentation exists and folowing any guidance 
contained in the documentation, e.g. manufacturer‘s in
.  
The operation they are being asked to supervise complies with the requirements of this Standards. Detailed 
advice on how they can ensure thi
s is given in various sections of this Standards. 
 
Al involved parties are aware that a diving operation is going to start or continue.  Obtain any necesary 
permision before starting or continuing the operation, normaly via a Permit to Work (PTW) syste
m.

Portable Electronic Devices
 
 
No person shal use a portable electronic device on board an aircraft that can adversely afect the performance 
of the aircraft. ADNOC Group authorized, as PAO shal ensure that apr
opriate instructions are in place for 
the cariage and use of portable electronic devices on board. 
 
 
Smoking and Alcohol Policy 
 
 
Smoking is prohibited at al times in any type of aircraft, on a helicopter landing area or within the boundary of 
aerodrome
. Smoking shal be controled to prevent unecesary risks for air transport operations. 
 
Personel under the influence of alcohol or drugs shal not be alowed to board any aircraft operating, for, or 
on behalf of the ADNOC 
Group
. 
 
Al personel shal co
mply with ADNOC smoking and alcohol policies. 
 
 
Carying Freight with Pasenger 
 
 
The cariage of freight in the cabin with the pasengers is permited within certain defined rules. Urgent freight, 
including mail bags, shal normaly be loaded in the freig
ht/bagage compartment. In exceptional 
circumstances only, it may be caried in the cabin with pasengers, but strictly subject to the folowing 
conditions: 
 
 
° 
It shal not obstruct main emergency exits or aces routes available to the pasengers
 
° 
It shal 
not obstruct secondary exit windows adjacent to ocupied seats
 
° 
It shal be securely tied down to aircraft strong points or with a tensioned net aceptable to the aircraft 
comander
 
° 
If seats are folded up, care shal be taken to ensure that they are stowed in such a way that they do 
not present a hazard to any pasenger in an emergency. 
 
 
Carying Dangerous Gods by Air 
 
 
Any substance and/article shal not be caried for air transpor
t under any circumstances that: 
 
  
° 
is liable to explode;
 
° 
can dangerously react;
 
° 
can produce a flame or dangerous evolution of heat or dangerous omision of toxic, corosive or 
flamable gases or vapours under conditions normaly encountered in transport
 
° 
is  prohibited by the GCA.
 
 
Prohibited or Restricted Articles 
 
 
In adition to restrictions on dangerous gods, other items/articles may be prohibited or restricted by the GCA 
and the UAE Ministry of Interior. These items include firearms, amunition, match
es, pesticides, weapons 
etc. Knives which are tols of trade (e.g. chefs and divers) shal be declared at check
- in. 
 
The cariage of cameras (including those in smart phones, laptops, tablets) and binoculars shal be restricted 
in acordance with oil fiel
d security requirements, unles specific permision has ben given prior to personel 
checking in for a flight. 
 
The list of items usualy prohibited by the GCA and/or UAE Ministry of Interior for cariage on aircraft or to 
ofshore instalations shal be
 refered to. This shal be displayed at dispatch counters for pasengers to view 
and read at al locations in Abu Dhabi such as al ADNOC Group air transport check in desks and al UAE 
based non
- ADNOC Group aerodromes/helicopter landing areas where flight
s to and from ADNOC areas 
originate.

ade available for inspection and verification by ADNOC / Group Companies prior to

reserves the right to inspect or audit such documents at any time to ensure the

The fitnes criteria set out in this document are the minimum requirements for ocupational health

ed by the nature of the job and its location.

exa
minations, periodic medical examination, referals for an ocupational health asesment for continuing

ional best practice for equivalent jobs can be refered to as criteria for asesment.

CHANGE OF JOB MEDICAL EXAMINATION
 
 
Change of job medical examinations shal be caried out at al times where the employe is asigned to a new 
job and/or to a new work environment. In this regard the change of job medical examination has special 
relevance where the new job and work environ
ment can pose significant risk to health and safety of the 
employe and others in the workplace. 
 
 
The fitnes for work shal be asesed against the general fitnes criteria described in this guideline and where 
there are specific requirements for the par
ticular job, against the aditional requirements for that post. 
 
 
REMOTE AREA & OFSHORE VISIT MEDICAL ASESMENT
 
 
If a Contractor has Department of Health (DOH) or relevant government authorities aproved medical 
facility/Clinic at site, medical screning
 procedure may be conducted at that medical facility/ Clinic.
 
 
Contractors and non
- company visitors requiring remote area/ofshore visits should have a valid fitnes to work 
certificate. They shal hence undergo aditional tests depending on the location o
r the purpose of the visit.
 
  
Simple medical screning procedure (e.g. physical examination, vital signs, visual acuity, history) may be 
adopted for individuals undertaking short
- duration visits (<24hrs) to remote and ofshore locations (Furnace 
et al, 20
8).
 
 
For those undertaking a remote/ofshore area visit longer than 24 hours, complete medical examination shal 
be conducted in acordance with this guideline.
 
 
ALCOHOL AND DRUG SCRENING
 
 
 
Where indicated, alcohol and drug screning shal be conducted 
in acordance with UAE Law and aplicable 
D epartment of 
Health
 (Previously Health Authority of Abu Dhabi (HAD) or location specific equivalent Health 
authority 
standards
 for screning and analytical detection methodologies for alcohol and drugs in biological 
samples (HAD, 2012). 
 
 
Pre
- employment / periodic Drug and Alcohol screning shal be completed on designated candidates (defined 
as a direct hire (periodic), new hir
e, or re
- hire) based on the technical work condition or job type. It may also 
be performed on personel performing safety critical jobs including fire fighter, Divers, seafarers, radiation 
workers, emergency responders etc. based on the risk asesment and
/or at the discretion of the concerned 
ADNOC Group to which the employe is afiliated. Al such requests should be forwarded to Group Medical 
Division wel in advance towards ensuring that adequate controls are in place for performing Drug and Alcohol 
scr
ening in acordance with UAE legislations, UAE Labor Law, aplicable Department of Health (DOH) 
standards
 and ADNOC policies and procedures.
 
  
In general aplicable provisions of Oil and Gas Contractor Drug and Alcohol Testing guidelines 
(2016) 
-  Internat
ional Asociation of Oil & Gas Producers shal be aplied for alcohol and Drug 
Screning. 
 
 
Group Medical Division shal reserve the right to release the results of said tests to the concerned Company 
or to any UAE governmental entity as per the aplicable
 law or if involved in a legal proceding / investigation 
conected with the test.

y.

Pre
- Employment Medical Examination 
 
 
Pre
- employment medical examinations shal be conducted for al r
ecruits to ensure that they are fit for the job 
and tasks to which they wil be asigned. The main objective is to ensure that the individual is fit to perform 
job tasks safely and effectively (without risk to thei
at they do not have 
clinical conditions that can be agravated by their work. 
 
 
Fitnes for work should be judged against the general fitnes criteria as per ADNOC Group requirements and 
against aditional requirements (if any) for that particular job. Thi
s principle shal also aply to other medical 
asesments outlined in the subsequent (folowing) sections.
 
 
Periodic Medical Examination
 
 
Periodic medical examinations shal be caried out for employes to ensure they remain fit for their job and 
that their health has not ben adversely afected by their work. It also enables recomendations to be made 
to acomodate any health problems as a
nd when aplicable. 
 
 
Unles otherwise required for specific job types (e.g. diver, firefighter, seafarer, radiation worker, fod 
handler, etc.) or by UAE Law for specific ocupational exposures and/or folowing ocupational health 
asesment, the minimum 
frequency of periodic medical examinations shal be as below:

- based workers age 50 and above

Ocupational History
 
 
The purpose of ocupational history taking is to:
 
 
a)
 
Identify ocupational risk factors for ilnes;
 
b)
 
Understand job demands;
 
c)
 
Advise on fitnes for work; and
 
d)
 
Advise on acomodation, adjustment, rehabilitation and redeployment. 
 
 
The folowing information should be recorded as a minimum requirement:
 
 
a)

b)

es and injuries

Present acute/chronic ocupational ilnes

and psychosocial); an
d  
f)  
History of industrial / ocupational disability
 
 
Family History
 
 
The family history outlines the age and health, or age and cause of death, of each imediate relative, including 
parents, grandparents, siblings, children, and grandchildren. The review of 
the family history includes the 
presence or absence in the family of hypertension, coronary artery disease, hyper/dyslipidaemia, stroke, 
diabetes, thyroid or renal disease, cancer, arthritis, tuberculosis, asthma or lung disease, headache, seizure 
disorder
, mental ilnes, suicide, alcohol or drug adiction, alergies, familial and genetic diseases as wel as 
symptoms reported by the candidate/employe.
 
 
The family history should include folowing as minimum:
 
 
a)
 
Heart disease or hypertension
 
b)
 
Alergies

 or inherited haemoglobinopathies

i)  
Diabetes
 
j)

k)
 
Mental disorders
 
l)  
Hepatitis

Fod Handlers
 
 
Fod handlers shal undergo a medical examination prior to undertaking work involving the preparation, 
coking, serving, and storage of fod. They should have god personal hygiene, be fre from relevant 
infections, and have a valid fod handler certificat
e isued by Abu Dhabi 
Agriculture and 
Fod 
Safety Authority 
( AD
A FSA
) (Previously ADFCA)
 
Fod handlers, who are returning to work from home country leave, should undergo medical 
asesment prior to resumption of duties
 
The examining physician should pay part
icular atention to presence of fever, jaundice, skin (hair, 
nails, hands, arms and face), 
and respiratory
 and gastrointestinal system disorders.
 
 
Periodicity of Medical Examination
 
 
Medical examination of fod handlers shal be conducted at pre
- employment
 stage and thereafter at least 
anualy. Where medical examinations are caried out by a third party healthcare provider, they should adhere 
to the recomendations in this guideline.
  
 
Medical examination
 
The minimum medical examination contents for fod h
andlers are as shown in table of this document. 
Aditional tests can be performed as determined by the examining physician based on the clinical indications.
  
 
Fitnes Criteria
 
 
Fod handlers
 with any of the folowing conditions shal be excluded from work in order to undergo further 
medical evaluation and treatment. They should not be considered fit for return to fod handling duties until 
demed non
- infectious. Examples of relevant infection
s include:
  
 
a.
 
Salmonela typhi, Salmonela paratyphi, Shigela sp
 
b.
 
Hepatitis A and E
 
c.  
Intestinal parasites (Entamoeba histolytica, Hokworms, Giardia)

NOC Clasification: Internal
 
d.
 
Active Pulmonary Tuberculosis
 
e.
 
Skin infections (e.g. Staph. aureus)
 
 
Fod handlers should be encouraged to 
report the folowing symptoms for further medical evaluation and 
treatment:
 
 
a.
 
Respiratory tract: fever, cough, colds, sore throat
 
b.
 
Gastrointestinal tract: fever, vomiting, diarhoea, jaundice; and
 
c.  
Skin: boils, infected wound, infected skin rash, especialy o
n hands or arms
 
 
Al cases of gastro
- enteritis or enteritis which may be infective shal be excluded from work until 48 hours after 
the cesation of diarhoea and vomiting. They may then return to work as long as they are clinicaly wel with 
normal wel
- f ormed stols (48 hour rule). For specific gastrointestinal infections, the opinion of an infectious 
disease specialist should be obtained regarding suitability to return to work.
 
 
Al fod handlers should be imunised against Hepatitis A, polio and typhoid
,  as per the frequency defined 
by Department of Health,
 if not previously vacinated.
  
 
Certificate of Fitnes for Work
 
 
Upon the completion of the medical examination, certificate of fitnes shal be isued by examining physician. 
The certificate should 
respect the medical confidentiality and should not provide any information specifying 
the clinical diagnosis or medical condition. The certificate should provide one of the folowing 
recomendations:
 
 
a.
 
Medicaly Fit for Job/location
 
b.
 
Medicaly Unfit for Job/
location
 
c.  
Temporarily Unfit for Job/location
 
d.
 
AGMC decision 
 
e.
 
Others 
 
 
Aditional advice such as suitability for specified work duties (taking into acount the job tasks/specifications) 
and the job location, recomendations on any workplace adjustments, 
restrictions or precautionary measures 
because of the existence of disease or disability, alowance for reduction of work hours where aplicable, and 
a time frame for subsequent folow
- up and review, may also be provided on the fitnes for work certificate
.  
 
For further details on fitnes criteria, Contractors shal refer to latest ADNOC Group Medical Fitnes 
Guidelines.

Contractors and their Sub

 shal comply with al aplicable UAE Laws, regulations and legal

 at their own cost and expense shal provide medical services (treatment, diagnostic investigations,
