MEDICAL EMERGENCY PREPAREDNES/PROVISIONS
 
 
Contractor shal ensure compliance with ADNOC and ADNOC Group Companies policies and
 procedures in 
dealing with medical emergencies.
 
 
General 
 
 
Contractor
s  shal develop and implement Medical Emergency Response Plan, including medical evacuation 
as part of the Facility Emergency Response Plan. 
 
 
Contractor
s  shal conduct emergency drils as per the established anual plan covering medical emergency 
scenarios.
 
 
Contractor
s  has the responsibility of cordinating medical emergency response to ensure seamles alignment 
with surounding industry and local emergen
cy services.
 
 
Contractor
s  shal have a writen procedure on Medical Evacuation (MEDEVAC) from its operational location 
to the nearest health service facility. 
 
 
It is the responsibility of the Contractor representative to establish contacts with the nearest health suport 
services and have a clear chain of comand for risk mitigation on
- site. 
 
 
The Medical Emergency Response procedure should at least contain the f
olowing: 
 
 
° 
The list of trained First aiders 
 
° 
Flow Chart of the proces for medical response and evacuation 
 
° 
The name and phone number of the ambulance services 
 
° 
Names and contacts of the On
- Scene Comanders
 
° 
Any other relevant information
 
 
ADNOC
 Group
 shal
l involve the contractor medical facilities and staf in its Emergency Preparednes 
Exercises /Drils where demed necesary. 
 
 
MINIMUM FIRST AID AND MEDICAL STAFING REQUIREMENTS
 
 
First Aid Requirements
 
 
First Aiders
 
 
First Aider must hold valid certificate from an acredited training provider as per Department of Health 
(previously HAD) 
standards
.  
 
Contractors whose number of workers does not exced 50 workers in one place, on country or a circle of 20 
km radius shal
 provide First aid kits at the workplace. 
 
 
Ratios of first aiders to number of employes must be in line with the work place environment and neds of 
the employer, employes and the requirements specific to the identified risks. The trained First aiders 
must 
be identified and designated to the role and apropriately distributed in the work place.
 
 
Table 3 provides recomendation to the optimum number of first aiders to be present in adition to any 
medicaly qualified profesi
onal in a work location.

le 3

Nurse/Paramedic
 
 
An employer whose number of workers in one place or within an area the radius of which is twenty kilometres, 
is exceding fifty (50) workers 
but
 les than two hundred
 workers in one place
, in adition to his obligation to 
provide first aid kits, 
shal
 employ a nurse holding a nursing certificate recognized by the Ministry of Health 
/DOH who shal be entrusted with rendering first aid treatment. 
 
 
However, contractors are permited to have 
a Service Level Agrement (SLA) with near
- by contractors, to 
share services of registered nurses/trained paramedics
 with prior aproval from ADNOC
 Group. 
 
 
Physician
 
 
Contractor
s  shal apoint a Physician to treat workers in a place determined by him for this purpose and to 
provide them with necesary drugs for treatment and al is fre of charge (Article 2, 3, 4 & 5 of Ministerial 
Resolution No. (37/2) of 1982 A.D on the medical 
care which the Contractors are obliged to provide to their 
workers). 
 
 
Contractor
s  shal have a Service Level Agrement (SLA) with an acredited medical facility within 20 KM 
radius and ensure the compliance with Department of Health aces requirements (i
.e. within 15 minutes 
(urban) or 19 minutes (rural) for provision of emergency medical services to cover al the work areas. 
 
 
ADNOC Group in consultation with ADNOC Medical / HSE may request provision of the onsite physician 
depending on the criticality 
of activities, camp / worksite location and number of employes.
 
 
First Aid Kit
 
 
C ontractor
s  shal provide first aid kit in an easily acesible location. 
 
 
The designated First
- aider should make sure that First
- aid boxes are always wel stocked and ready 
for use 
in the case of an emergency.
 
 
Al employes shal be made aware of the designated First Aiders in the workplace. First Aiders name and 
telephone number should be displayed in the visible area.
 
 
Visible signage should be displayed identifying locati
on of the First Aid Kit and CPR/AED Equipment. First Aid 
kit contents should be finalized in consideration of the setings, scope of service and in reference to local & 
Federal Regulatory Framework, Codes of Practice, international best practices and with 
due consideration on 
the area and/or risk identified. 
 
 
The First
- aid kit shal be wel stocked with suficient items. An inventory of the First
- aid kit should be displayed 
near the First
- aid box. The First
- aid box contents should be checked regularly, at 
least every month, and 
maintained on a regular basis by the designated First
- aider. Particular note should be made to verify and 
remove in time, items that are nearing their expiry date.

- aid kit has clear ins
truction leaflets or guides available that can

of emergency and/or first aid personel shal be displayed on the dor of the first aid rom/s
ick bay.
 
 
AUTOMATED EXTERNAL DEFIBRILATORS (AED) 
 
 
(a)
 
Employers should consider establishing an AED program and instaling AEDs to manage suden 
cardiac arest in their workplace, based on risk asesment and /or as mandated by relevant 
authorities. 
 
(b)
 
An AED 
program should: 
 
(i)
 
Include a management system and writen sumary of the AED program, establishing clearly 
defined lines of responsibility for those who overse and monitor the program or participate 
in it; 
 
(i)
 
Asign a licensed
 health care profesional
 for ma
naging al medical aspects of the AED 
program and oversee the program‘s administrati
 
(i)
 
Be integrated in the more general plan describing emergency responses at the ocupational 
seting; 
 
(iv)
 
Include recognized and 
standardized
 training of al designated first aid responders and 
employed healthcare profesionals. Topics should include CPR and use of the specific AED 
expected to be available and
 used at the ocupational sit
 
Note: In case of suden cardiac arest, AEDs should be used by the first available suitably 
trained first aider. 
 
(v)
 
Ensure selection and placement of AEDs and ancilary suplies that met local and federal 
legislation criteria for medical devices, and ensure
 they are regularly maintained as per the 
manufacturer‘s requirements; and 
 
(vi)
 
be incorporated into, or have its own quality asurance program. 
 
 
(c)
 
Placement of AEDs should be considered for al workplaces where people may congregate, such as 
corporate ofices,
 worksites, and employe camps/vilages, in particular in the folowing areas: 
 
(i)
 
Areas where many people work closely together, such as asembly lines and ofice 
buildings; 
 
(i)
 
Close to a confined space; 
 
(i)
 
Areas where electric
- powered devices are used; 
 
(iv)
 
Outdor
 worksites where lightning may ocur; 
 
(v)
 
Health units where workers may sek treatment for heart atack symptoms; 
 
(vi)
 
Company fitnes units and cafeterias; and 
 
(vi)
 
Remote sites, such as of
- shore driling rigs, construction projects, marine vesels, power 
transmis
sion lines, and energy pipe lines. 
 
(d)
 
AEDs should be conveniently instaled and easily acesible to ensure response time within 3
- 5 
minutes.
 
(e)
 
The 
First
- aider/ Certified Nurse/ Physician shal always record details of any injured person they treat. 
It is impo
rtant that the folowing information be writen in a register:
